- **Black:** king e8, queen d8, rooks a8 and h8, bishops a3 and e4, knights c4 and f6, pawns e2, f4, a6, b7, c7, f7, g7, h7.

- b1 is covered by my bishop on e4 (along e4–d3–c2–b1).

- **Block on b1 or c1?** No white piece can reach either square.
- **Move the king?** It has no safe squares.

So exd1=Q is checkmate.

Player_Black (to Proxy):

The move is **Qd4 (d8d4)**.

The queen goes to d4 with nothing blocking her path (d7, d6 and d5 are empty). There she makes two threats:

1. **Qxf2#.** The bishop on c5 supports the queen on f2, so the king can't take her, and d1 is blocked by White's own queen.
2. **Qxa1**, winning the undefended rook along the empty c3–b2 diagonal.

White's possible replies:
- **Nf3?** attacks the queen but doesn't guard f2, so Qxf2# follows.
- **Bxb4?** takes the knight but still allows Qxf2#.
- **e3** or **Nh3** stops the mate, but then Qxa1 wins the rook.

- **King can't take:** Once the queen leaves d4, the bishop on c5 defends f2 along c5–d4–e3–f2. So the king cannot capture.
- **No escape squares:** d1, f1, d2 and e2 are all filled by white's own pieces.
- **No other capture:** No white piece attacks f2. The knight on g1 covers e2, f3 and h3, not f2.
- **No block:** The queen is right next to the king, so nothing can be put in between.

make_move d4f2
